1→"""Per-stage instruction text blocks for triage prompts."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def _observe_instructions() -> str:
     7→    return """\
     8→## OBSERVE Stage Instructions
     9→
    10→Your task: verify every open review issue against the actual source code.
    11→
    12→**The review system has a high false-positive rate.** Issues frequently:
    13→- Claim "12 unsafe casts" when there are actually 2
    14→- Describe code that was already refactored
    15→- Propose over-engineering that would make things worse
    16→- Count props/returns/args wrong
    17→
    18→Your job is to catch these. An observe report that just restates issue titles is **worthless**.
    19→The value you add is reading the actual code and forming an independent judgment.
    20→
    21→Do NOT analyze themes, strategy, or relationships between issues. That's the next stage (reflect).
    22→Just verify: is each issue real?
    23→
    24→**CRITICAL: You must cite specific issue IDs (hash prefixes like [abcd1234]) in your report.**
    25→The confirmation gate requires citing at least 10% of issues (or 5, whichever is smaller).
    26→
    27→**USE SUBAGENTS to parallelize this work.** Launch parallel subagents — one per dimension
    28→group — to investigate concurrently. Each subagent MUST:
    29→- Open and read the actual source file for EVERY assigned issue
    30→- Verify specific claims: count the actual casts, props, returns, line count
    31→- Check if the suggested fix already exists (common false positive)
    32→- Report a clear verdict per issue: genuine / false positive / exaggerated / over-engineering
    33→
    34→Example subagent split for 90 issues across 17 dimensions:
    35→- Subagent 1: architecture + organization (cross_module_architecture, package_organization, high_level_elegance)
    36→- Subagent 2: abstraction + design (abstraction_fitness, design_coherence, mid_level_elegance)
    37→- Subagent 3: duplicates + contracts (contract_coherence, api_surface_coherence, low_level_elegance)
    38→- Subagent 4: migrations + debt + conventions (incomplete_migration, ai_generated_debt, convention_outlier, naming_quality)
    39→- Subagent 5: type safety + errors + tests (type_safety, error_consistency, test_strategy, initialization_coupling, dependency_health)
    40→
    41→**What a GOOD observe report looks like:**
    42→- "[34580232] taskType is plain string — FALSE POSITIVE. Uses branded string union KnownTaskType
    43→  with ~25 literals in src/types/database.ts line 50. The issue describes code that doesn't exist."
    44→- "[b634fc71] useGenerationsPaneController returns 60+ values — GENUINE. Confirmed 65 properties
    45→  at lines 217-282. Mixes pane lifecycle, filters, gallery data, interaction, and navigation."
    46→
    47→**What a LAZY observe report looks like (will be rejected):**
    48→- "There are several convention issues that should be addressed"
    49→- "The type safety dimension has some genuine concerns"
    50→- Listing issue titles without any verification or independent analysis
    51→
    52→**Your report must include for EVERY issue:**
    53→1. The hash prefix
    54→2. Your verdict (genuine / false positive / exaggerated / over-engineering)
    55→3. The specific evidence (what you found when you read the code)
    56→
    57→When done, run:
    58→```
    59→desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "<your analysis with [hash] citations>"
    60→```
    61→"""
    62→
    63→
    64→def _reflect_instructions() -> str:
    65→    return """\
    66→## REFLECT Stage Instructions
    67→
    68→Your task: using the verdicts from observe, design the cluster structure.
    69→
    70→**A strategy is NOT a restatement of observe.** Observe says "here's what I found." Reflect
    71→says "here's what we should DO about it, and here's what we should NOT do, and here's WHY."
    72→
    73→### What you must do:
    74→
    75→1. **Filter:** which issues are genuine (from observe verdicts)?
    76→2. **Map:** for each genuine issue, what file/directory does it touch?
    77→3. **Group:** which issues share files or directories? These become clusters.
    78→4. **Skip:** which issues should be skipped? (with per-issue justification — "low priority" is
    79→   not a justification; "the fix would add a 50-line abstraction to save 3 lines of duplication" is)
    80→5. **Order:** which clusters depend on others? What's the execution sequence?
    81→6. **Check recurring patterns** — compare current issues against resolved history. If the same
    82→   dimension keeps producing issues, that's a root cause that needs addressing, not just
    83→   another round of fixes.
    84→
    85→### Your report MUST include a concrete cluster blueprint
    86→
    87→This blueprint is what the organize stage will execute. Be specific:
    88→```
    89→Cluster "media-lightbox-hooks": issues X, Y, Z (all in src/domains/media-lightbox/)
    90→Cluster "task-typing": issues A, B (both touch src/types/database.ts)
    91→Skip: issue W (false positive per observe), issue V (over-engineering — fix adds 50 lines for 3 lines saved)
    92→```
    93→
    94→### What a LAZY reflect looks like (will be rejected):
    95→- Restating observe findings in slightly different words
    96→- "We should prioritize high-impact items and defer low-priority ones"
    97→- A bulleted list of dimensions without any strategic thinking
    98→- Ignoring recurring patterns
    99→- No cluster blueprint (just vague grouping ideas)
   100→
   101→### What a GOOD reflect looks like:
   102→- "50% false positive rate. Of 34 issues, 17 are genuine. 10 of those are batch-scriptable
   103→  convention fixes (zero risk, 30 min) — cluster 'convention-batch'. The remaining 7 split into
   104→  3 clusters by file proximity: 'media-lightbox-hooks' (issues X,Y,Z — all in src/domains/media-lightbox/),
   105→  'timeline-cleanup' (issues A,B,C — touching Timeline components), 'task-typing' (issues D,E).
   106→  Skip: issue W (false positive), issue V (over-engineering).
   107→  design_coherence recurs (2 resolved, 5 open) but only 1 of the 5 actually warrants work."
   108→
   109→When done, run:
   110→```
   111→desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "<your strategy with cluster blueprint>" --attestation "<80+ chars mentioning dimensions or recurring patterns>"
   112→```
   113→"""
   114→
   115→
   116→def _organize_instructions() -> str:
   117→    return """\
   118→## ORGANIZE Stage Instructions
   119→
   120→Your task: execute the cluster blueprint from the reflect stage.
   121→
   122→The reflect report contains a specific plan: which clusters to create, which issues go
   123→where, what to skip. Build it using the CLI. If something doesn't work as planned
   124→(issue hash doesn't match, file proximity doesn't hold), adjust and document why.
   125→
   126→This stage should be largely mechanical. If you find yourself making major strategic
   127→decisions, something went wrong in reflect — the strategy should already be decided.
   128→
   129→### Process
   130→
   131→1. Review the reflect report's cluster blueprint (provided below)
   132→2. **Skip false positives and over-engineering** identified in observe/reflect. Every skip needs a
   133→   per-issue justification — not "low priority" but "false positive: the code at line 47
   134→   already uses named constants, contradicting the issue's claim":
   135→   ```
   136→   desloppify plan skip --permanent <pattern> --note "<specific per-issue reason>" --attest "<attestation>"
   137→   ```
   138→3. Create clusters as specified in the blueprint:
   139→   `desloppify plan cluster create <name> --description "..."`
   140→4. Add issues: `desloppify plan cluster add <name> <patterns...>`
   141→5. Add steps that consolidate: one step per file or logical change, NOT one step per issue
   142→6. Set `--effort` on each step individually (trivial/small/medium/large)
   143→7. Set `--depends-on` when clusters touch overlapping files
   144→
   145→### Quality gates (the confirmation will check these)
   146→
   147→Before recording, verify:
   148→- [ ] Every cluster name describes an area or specific change, not a problem type
   149→- [ ] No cluster has issues from 5+ unrelated directories (theme-group smell)
   150→- [ ] Step count < issue count (consolidation happened)
```

1→"""Subprocess-oriented runner helpers for review batch execution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from .runner_process_impl.attempts import (
    10→    _handle_early_attempt_return,
    11→    _handle_failed_attempt,
    12→    _handle_successful_attempt,
    13→    _handle_timeout_or_stall,
    14→    _resolve_retry_config,
    15→    _run_batch_attempt,
    16→)
    17→from .runner_process_impl.io import _extract_payload_from_log  # noqa: F401 (runner_parallel import)
    18→from .runner_process_impl.types import (
    19→    CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    20→    FollowupScanDeps,
    21→)
    22→
    23→
    24→def codex_batch_command(*, prompt: str, repo_root: Path, output_file: Path) -> list[str]:
    25→    """Build one codex exec command line for a batch prompt."""
    26→    effort = os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_CODEX_REASONING_EFFORT", "low").strip().lower()
    27→    if effort not in {"low", "medium", "high", "xhigh"}:
    28→        effort = "low"
    29→    return [
    30→        "codex",
    31→        "exec",
    32→        "--ephemeral",
    33→        "-C",
    34→        str(repo_root),
    35→        "-s",
    36→        "workspace-write",
    37→        "-c",
    38→        'approval_policy="never"',
    39→        "-c",
    40→        f'model_reasoning_effort="{effort}"',
    41→        "-o",
    42→        str(output_file),
    43→        prompt,
    44→    ]
    45→
    46→
    47→def run_codex_batch(
    48→    *,
    49→    prompt: str,
    50→    repo_root: Path,
    51→    output_file: Path,
    52→    log_file: Path,
    53→    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    54→    codex_batch_command_fn=None,
    55→) -> int:
    56→    """Execute one codex batch and return a stable CLI-style status code."""
    57→    if codex_batch_command_fn is None:
    58→        codex_batch_command_fn = codex_batch_command
    59→    cmd = codex_batch_command_fn(
    60→        prompt=prompt,
    61→        repo_root=repo_root,
    62→        output_file=output_file,
    63→    )
    64→    config = _resolve_retry_config(deps)
    65→    log_sections: list[str] = []
    66→
    67→    for attempt in range(1, config.max_attempts + 1):
    68→        header, result = _run_batch_attempt(
    69→            cmd=cmd,
    70→            deps=deps,
    71→            output_file=output_file,
    72→            log_file=log_file,
    73→            log_sections=log_sections,
    74→            attempt=attempt,
    75→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
    76→            use_popen=config.use_popen,
    77→            live_log_interval=config.live_log_interval,
    78→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
    79→        )
    80→        early_return = _handle_early_attempt_return(result)
    81→        if early_return is not None:
    82→            return early_return
    83→        timeout_or_stall = _handle_timeout_or_stall(
    84→            header=header,
    85→            result=result,
    86→            deps=deps,
    87→            output_file=output_file,
    88→            log_file=log_file,
    89→            log_sections=log_sections,
    90→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
    91→        )
    92→        if timeout_or_stall is not None:
    93→            if timeout_or_stall == 0:
    94→                return 0  # recovered from timeout/stall
    95→            # Non-recovered timeout/stall: retry if attempts remain
    96→            if attempt < config.max_attempts:
    97→                delay = config.retry_backoff_seconds * (2 ** (attempt - 1))
    98→                log_sections.append(
    99→                    f"Timeout/stall on attempt {attempt}/{config.max_attempts}; "
   100→                    f"retrying in {delay:.1f}s."
   101→                )
   102→                if delay > 0:
   103→                    deps.sleep_fn(delay)
   104→                continue
   105→            return timeout_or_stall
   106→
   107→        log_sections.append(
   108→            f"{header}\n\nSTDOUT:\n{result.stdout_text}\n\nSTDERR:\n{result.stderr_text}\n"
   109→        )
   110→
   111→        success_code = _handle_successful_attempt(
   112→            result=result,
   113→            output_file=output_file,
   114→            log_file=log_file,
   115→            deps=deps,
   116→            log_sections=log_sections,
   117→        )
   118→        if success_code is not None:
   119→            return success_code
   120→        failure_code = _handle_failed_attempt(
   121→            result=result,
   122→            deps=deps,
   123→            attempt=attempt,
   124→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
   125→            retry_backoff_seconds=config.retry_backoff_seconds,
   126→            log_file=log_file,
   127→            log_sections=log_sections,
   128→        )
   129→        if failure_code is not None:
   130→            return failure_code
   131→
   132→    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
   133→    return 1
   134→
   135→
   136→def run_followup_scan(
   137→    *,
   138→    lang_name: str,
   139→    scan_path: str,
   140→    deps: FollowupScanDeps,
   141→    force_queue_bypass: bool = False,
   142→) -> int:
   143→    """Run a follow-up scan and return a non-zero status when it fails."""
   144→    scan_cmd = [
   145→        deps.python_executable,
   146→        "-m",
   147→        "desloppify",
   148→        "--lang",
   149→        lang_name,
   150→        "scan",

   103→    """Run epic triage: staged workflow OBSERVE → REFLECT → ORGANIZE → ENRICH → COMMIT."""
   104→    resolved_services = _build_triage_services()

1→"""Parallel execution and progress-callback helpers for review batches."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import threading
     8→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    12→
    13→from .execution import (
    14→    _drain_parallel_completions,
    15→    _execute_serial,
    16→    _queue_parallel_tasks,
    17→    _resolve_parallel_runtime,
    18→)
    19→from .progress import _coerce_batch_execution_options
    20→from .types import (
    21→    BatchExecutionOptions,
    22→    BatchProgressEvent,
    23→    BatchResult,
    24→    BatchTask,
    25→)
    26→from ..runner_process import _extract_payload_from_log
    27→
    28→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    29→
    30→
    31→def execute_batches(
    32→    *,
    33→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
    34→    options: BatchExecutionOptions | None = None,
    35→    progress_fn=None,
    36→    error_log_fn=None,
    37→) -> list[int]:
    38→    """Run indexed tasks and return failed index list.
    39→
    40→    Each value in *tasks* is a zero-arg callable returning an int exit code.
    41→    All domain knowledge (files, prompts, etc.) is pre-bound by the caller.
    42→    """
    43→    resolved_options = _coerce_batch_execution_options(options)
    44→    contract_cache: dict[int, str] = {}
    45→    indexes = sorted(tasks)
    46→    if resolved_options.run_parallel:
    47→        max_workers, heartbeat = _resolve_parallel_runtime(
    48→            indexes=indexes,
    49→            max_parallel_workers=resolved_options.max_parallel_workers,
    50→            heartbeat_seconds=resolved_options.heartbeat_seconds,
    51→        )
    52→        failures: set[int] = set()
    53→        progress_failures: set[int] = set()
    54→        started_at: dict[int, float] = {}
    55→        lock = threading.Lock()
    56→        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as executor:
    57→            futures = _queue_parallel_tasks(
    58→                executor=executor,
    59→                indexes=indexes,
    60→                tasks=tasks,
    61→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
    62→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    63→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
    64→                max_workers=max_workers,
    65→                failures=failures,
    66→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
    67→                started_at=started_at,
    68→                lock=lock,
    69→                clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
    70→            )
    71→            pending = set(futures.keys())
    72→            _drain_parallel_completions(
    73→                pending=pending,
    74→                futures=futures,
    75→                heartbeat=heartbeat,
    76→                indexes=indexes,
    77→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
    78→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    79→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
    80→                failures=failures,
    81→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
    82→                started_at=started_at,
    83→                lock=lock,
    84→                clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
    85→            )
    86→        return sorted(failures)
    87→    return _execute_serial(
    88→        tasks=tasks,
    89→        indexes=indexes,
    90→        progress_fn=progress_fn,
    91→        error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    92→        clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
    93→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
    94→    )
    95→
    96→
    97→def collect_batch_results(
    98→    *,
    99→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   100→    failures: list[int],
   101→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
   102→    allowed_dims: set[str],
   103→    extract_payload_fn,
   104→    normalize_result_fn,
   105→) -> tuple[list[BatchResult], list[int]]:
   106→    """Parse and normalize batch outputs, preserving prior failures."""
   107→    batch_results: list[BatchResult] = []
   108→    failure_set = set(failures)
   109→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   110→        had_execution_failure = idx in failure_set
   111→        raw_path = output_files[idx]
   112→        payload = None
   113→        parsed_from_log = False
   114→        if raw_path.exists():
   115→            try:
   116→                payload = extract_payload_fn(raw_path.read_text())
   117→            except OSError as exc:
   118→                logger.warning("Failed reading batch payload %s: %s", raw_path, exc)
   119→                payload = None
   120→        if payload is None:
   121→            payload = _extract_payload_from_log(idx, raw_path, extract_payload_fn)
   122→            parsed_from_log = payload is not None
   123→        if payload is None:
   124→            failure_set.add(idx)
   125→            continue
   126→        if parsed_from_log:
   127→            try:
   128→                safe_write_text(raw_path, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   129→            except OSError as exc:
   130→                logger.warning("Failed writing normalized batch payload %s: %s", raw_path, exc)
   131→        try:
   132→            (
   133→                assessments,
   134→                issues,
   135→                dimension_notes,
   136→                dimension_judgment,
   137→                quality,
   138→            ) = normalize_result_fn(payload, allowed_dims)
   139→        except ValueError as exc:
   140→            logger.debug("Invalid batch payload at index %s (%s): %s", idx, raw_path, exc)
   141→            failure_set.add(idx)
   142→            continue
   143→        if had_execution_failure:
   144→            failure_set.discard(idx)
   145→            logger.info(
   146→                "Batch #%d recovered: execution exited non-zero but raw payload parsed successfully",
   147→                idx + 1,
   148→            )
   149→        batch_results.append(
   150→            BatchResult(
   151→                batch_index=idx + 1,
   152→                assessments=assessments,
   153→                dimension_notes=dimension_notes,
   154→                dimension_judgment=dimension_judgment,
   155→                issues=issues,
   156→                quality=quality,
   157→            )
   158→        )
   159→    return batch_results, sorted(failure_set)
   160→
   161→
   162→__all__ = [
   163→    "BatchResult",
   164→    "BatchExecutionOptions",
   165→    "BatchProgressEvent",
   166→    "collect_batch_results",
   167→    "execute_batches",
   168→]
   169→

    34→    normalized_indexes: list[int] = []
    35→    seen: set[int] = set()
    36→    for idx in indexes:
    37→        if idx in seen:
    38→            continue
    39→        seen.add(idx)
    40→        if idx not in tasks:
    41→            continue
    42→        normalized_indexes.append(idx)
    43→    if not normalized_indexes:
    44→        return []
    45→    return execute_serial_tasks(
    46→        tasks=tasks,
    47→        indexes=normalized_indexes,
    48→        progress_fn=progress_fn,
    49→        error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    50→        clock_fn=clock_fn,
    51→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
    52→        emit_progress_fn=_emit_progress,
    53→        record_execution_error_fn=_record_execution_error,
    54→        runner_task_exceptions=_RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS,
    55→    )
    56→
    57→
    58→def _resolve_parallel_runtime(
    59→    *,
    60→    indexes: list[int],
    61→    max_parallel_workers,
    62→    heartbeat_seconds,
    63→) -> tuple[int, float | None]:
    64→    requested = (
    65→        int(max_parallel_workers)
    66→        if isinstance(max_parallel_workers, int) and max_parallel_workers > 0
    67→        else 8
    68→    )
    69→    max_workers = max(1, min(len(indexes), requested))
    70→    heartbeat = (
    71→        float(heartbeat_seconds)
    72→        if isinstance(heartbeat_seconds, int | float) and heartbeat_seconds > 0
    73→        else None
    74→    )
    75→    return max_workers, heartbeat
    76→
    77→
    78→def _run_parallel_task(
    79→    *,
    80→    idx: int,
    81→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
    82→    progress_fn,
    83→    error_log_fn,
    84→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
    85→    max_workers: int,
    86→    progress_failures: set[int],
    87→    started_at: dict[int, float],
    88→    lock: threading.Lock,
    89→    clock_fn,
    90→) -> int:
    91→    with lock:
    92→        started_at[idx] = float(clock_fn())
    93→    progress_error = _emit_progress(
    94→        progress_fn,
    95→        idx,
    96→        "start",
    97→        None,
    98→        details={"max_workers": max_workers},
    99→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
   100→    )
```

## Comprehensive Exploration: Desloppify Triage Runner System

I've thoroughly explored the triage runner system. Here's what I found:

### 1. **Core Architecture Overview**

The triage runner system is a **multi-stage AI agent orchestration framework** that coordinates multiple AI agents (Codex and Claude) to complete a 5-stage triage process. The system is designed to be:
- **Modular**: Each stage has dedicated orchestrators and prompt builders
- **Agent-agnostic**: Two distinct runner patterns (Codex subprocess pipeline vs. Claude manual orchestration)
- **Fault-tolerant**: Built-in validation, retry logic, and attestation mechanisms
- **Parallel-capable**: Can execute multiple batches concurrently with thread pooling

**Key entry point:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage_handlers.py` (line 102-131) via `cmd_plan_triage()`

---

### 2. **Two Runner Modes**

#### **Mode 1: Codex Pipeline (Automated, Subprocess-based)**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline.py`

- Runs all 5 stages (`observe` → `reflect` → `organize` → `enrich` → `sense-check`) automatically
- Each stage executes via subprocess (using Codex) with full automation
- Handles validation, confirmation, and progression automatically
- **Dispatch mechanism:**
  - Invoked when `--runner codex` flag is set
  - Calls `run_codex_pipeline(args, stages_to_run=..., services=...)`
  - Runs stages sequentially, skipping already-confirmed stages (resumable)

**Key flow (lines 366-536):**
1. Loads/validates plan via TriageServices
2. Creates run directory with timestamps (`.desloppify/triage_runs/{stamp}/`)
3. For each stage:
   - Check if already confirmed → skip
   - **Execute stage** via `_execute_stage()` (parallel or single execution)
   - **Validate stage** via `validate_stage()` 
   - **Confirm stage** via auto-attestation
4. Auto-completes triage if all stages pass
5. Writes `run_summary.json` with results

#### **Mode 2: Claude Orchestrator (Manual, Interactive)**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_claude.py`

- Prints instructions for Claude Code agent to manually orchestrate triage
- Claude launches **sub-subagents** for each stage
- Each stage runs as a separate agent tool invocation
- **Dispatch mechanism:**
  - Invoked when `--runner claude` flag is set
  - Calls `run_claude_orchestrator(args, services=...)`
  - Just prints instructions (no actual execution by orchestrator)

**Key instructions (lines 27-45):**
- One subagent per stage (5 total)
- Observe stage uses sub-subagents per dimension group
- Enrich stage uses sub-subagents per cluster
- Sense-check launches TWO parallel subagents (content + structure)
- Between stages: verify via dashboard, confirm

---

### 3. **Agent Runner Interface & Contract**

An agent runner (like Codex) must implement the following contract:

**Function signature:**
```python
run_triage_stage(
    *,
    prompt: str,              # Complete stage prompt
    repo_root: Path,          # Repository root for context
    output_file: Path,        # Where to write agent output
    log_file: Path,           # Where to write execution logs
    timeout_seconds: int,     # Max execution time (default 1800s)
    validate_output_fn: Callable[[Path], bool] | None,  # Output validation
) -> int:  # Exit code (0=success, non-0=failure)
```

**Implementation:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/codex_runner.py`

The `run_triage_stage()` function:
1. Normalizes/validates the prompt
2. Builds a Codex subprocess command via `codex_batch_command()` (lines 24-44)
3. Wraps execution with `CodexBatchRunnerDeps` (timeout, retry, logging config)
4. Calls `run_codex_batch()` from shared review runner infrastructure
5. Returns exit code (0 if output file has text and passes validation)

**Codex command structure** (lines 29-43):
```bash
codex exec --ephemeral -C {repo_root} -s workspace-write \
  -c 'approval_policy="never"' \
  -c 'model_reasoning_effort="low|medium|high|xhigh"' \
  -o {output_file} \
  {prompt}
```

---

### 4. **Orchestrator Pattern: Stage Handlers & Parallel Execution**

**Stage handlers** provide **pluggable execution/recording hooks** for each stage.

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline.py` (lines 55-152)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class StageHandler:
    run_parallel: Callable[..., tuple[bool | None, str | None]] | None = None
    record_report: Callable[[str, argparse.Namespace, TriageServices], None] | None = None
```

**Registered handlers** (lines 143-152):
- **`observe`**: Uses `run_observe()` (parallel by dimension group)
- **`sense-check`**: Uses `run_sense_check()` (parallel by cluster + 1 structure batch)
- **Other stages** (`reflect`, `organize`, `enrich`): Use standard single-stage execution

#### **Parallel Execution Model**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_parallel/__init__.py`

```python
execute_batches(
    tasks: dict[int, object],        # {batch_index: Callable[[], int]}
    options: BatchExecutionOptions,  # parallel, max_workers, heartbeat
    progress_fn: Callable,           # Status callbacks (start/done/heartbeat)
    error_log_fn: Callable           # Error logging
) -> list[int]:  # Failed batch indexes
```

**Thread pool execution:**
- `max_workers`: Determined by `min(num_tasks, max_parallel_workers)` (default 8)
- Each task is a zero-argument callable returning an exit code
- Progress callbacks emitted: `start`, `done` (with elapsed/code), `heartbeat` (every 15s)
- Failures are collected and returned

#### **Observe Stage Parallelization** 
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_observe.py`

Creates one batch per dimension group (lines 41-128):
- Groups issues by dimension
- Each batch gets a prompt via `build_observe_batch_prompt()`
- Outputs stored in `.desloppify/triage_runs/{stamp}/output/observe_batch_{i}.raw.txt`
- Merged after all complete via `_merge_observe_outputs()`

#### **Sense-Check Parallelization**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_sense.py`

Launches 2 concurrent subagent types (lines 25-135):
1. **Content verification batches**: One per cluster (line 51-74)
   - Prompt: `build_sense_check_content_prompt()` — verifies step details against source
2. **Structure batch**: One global (line 76-96)
   - Prompt: `build_sense_check_structure_prompt()` — checks cross-cluster dependencies

---

### 5. **Prompt Architecture**

All prompts follow a **modular composition pattern** in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/stage_prompts.py` (lines 29-63):

```
1. PREAMBLE (from stage_prompts_instruction_shared.py)
2. ISSUE DATA (from engine.plan.build_triage_prompt())
3. PRIOR STAGE REPORTS (if available)
4. STAGE-SPECIFIC INSTRUCTIONS (from stage_prompts_instruction_blocks.py)
5. CLI REFERENCE (command examples)
6. VALIDATION REQUIREMENTS (stage-specific gates)
```

#### **Prompt Building Files:**
- **`stage_prompts_instruction_shared.py`**: Common blocks (`_PREAMBLE`, `_CLI_REFERENCE`, `_STAGES`)
- **`stage_prompts_instruction_blocks.py`**: Per-stage instructions (5 functions):
  - `_observe_instructions()`: Verify issues, cite specific hashes, use sub-subagents per dimension
  - `_reflect_instructions()`: Design cluster structure, strategize skips/ordering
  - `_organize_instructions()`: Create clusters, add issues, set dependencies
  - `_enrich_instructions()`: Add executable step details, effort tags, issue refs
  - `_sense_check_instructions()`: (not read yet, but structured same way)
- **`stage_prompts_observe.py`**: `build_observe_batch_prompt()` for per-dimension-group batches
- **`stage_prompts_sense.py`**: 
  - `build_sense_check_content_prompt()` (lines 8-100) — per-cluster content verification
  - `build_sense_check_structure_prompt()` (lines 103-171) — global dependency checking
- **`stage_prompts_validation.py`**: `_validation_requirements()` function for stage gates

#### **Key Prompt Features:**

**Preamble** (`stage_prompts_instruction_shared.py` lines 7-35):
- Sets expectations: read actual source code, verify claims, don't take shortcuts
- Warns: "Only run commands for YOUR stage ({stage}). Do NOT re-run earlier stages."
- Emphasizes: "Use the desloppify CLI to record your work."

**Sense-Check Content** (lines 8-100):
- Checks **per-step**: line numbers, names, counts, staleness, vagueness, effort tags, duplicates, over-engineering
- Can run: `desloppify plan cluster update {name} --update-step N --detail ... --effort ...`
- **Cannot run**: reorder steps, add depends-on, add cascade steps (structure subagent handles)

**Sense-Check Structure** (lines 103-171):
- Builds file-touch graph per cluster
- Checks: shared files, missing cascade, circular deps
- Can run: `--depends-on`, `--add-step` (dependencies and structure only)
- **Cannot run**: modify step details, change effort, deduplicate, run other commands

---

### 6. **Stage Validation & Attestation**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/stage_validation.py`

#### **Per-Stage Validation**
`validate_stage(stage, plan, state, repo_root, triage_input)` (lines 104-205):

| Stage | Requirements |
|-------|-------------|
| `observe` | Report exists & ≥100 chars, cites 5 issues or 10% of total (whichever smaller) |
| `reflect` | Report exists & ≥100 chars |
| `organize` | Manual clusters exist, all enriched, no unclustered issues. Warns: overlaps, scatter, ratios |
| `enrich` | Clusters exist, all steps have detail, effort, issue_refs, paths exist (quality checks) |
| `sense-check` | Report exists & ≥100 chars, plus all enrich-level checks |

#### **Auto-Attestation**
`build_auto_attestation(stage, plan, triage_input)` (lines 261-314):

Generates stage-appropriate attestations that reference real dimension/cluster names:
- **Observe**: "I have thoroughly analysed {N} issues across dimensions including {top 3 dims}..."
- **Reflect**: "My strategy accounts for {N} issues across dimensions including {top 3}..."
- **Organize**: "I have organized all review issues into clusters including {top 3}..."
- **Enrich**: "Steps in clusters including {top 3} are executor-ready with detail, file paths..."
- **Sense-check**: "Content and structure verified for clusters including {top 3}..."

#### **Quality Checks**
`run_enrich_quality_checks(plan, repo_root, phase_label)` (lines 32-101):

Checks for:
- Underspecified steps (missing detail or issue_refs)
- Missing file paths on disk
- Missing effort tags
- Missing issue_refs
- Vague detail (<80 chars, no file paths)

---

### 7. **TriageServices Dependency Bundle**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/services.py`

A frozen dataclass that bundles callables used across triage modules (lines 43-54):

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TriageServices:
    command_runtime: Callable[[argparse.Namespace], CommandRuntime]
    load_plan: Callable[[], PlanModel]
    save_plan: Callable[[PlanModel], None]
    collect_triage_input: Callable[[PlanModel, StateModel], TriageInput]
    detect_recurring_patterns: Callable[...]
    append_log_entry: AppendLogEntryFn
    extract_issue_citations: Callable[[str, set[str]], set[str]]
    build_triage_prompt: Callable[[TriageInput], str]
```

**Advantage**: Allows easy mocking/patching in tests. Every triage module accepts optional `services: TriageServices | None` parameter and falls back to `default_triage_services()`.

---

### 8. **Run Artifacts & Logging**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline.py` (lines 381-398)

Structure:
```
.desloppify/triage_runs/{YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS}/
├── prompts/
│   ├── observe.md
│   ├── observe_batch_0.md, observe_batch_1.md, ...
│   ├── reflect.md
│   ├── organize.md
│   ├── enrich.md
│   ├── sense_check_content_0.md, sense_check_content_1.md, ...
│   └── sense_check_structure.md
├── output/
│   ├── observe.raw.txt (or observe_batch_*.raw.txt)
│   ├── reflect.raw.txt
│   └── ... (one per stage)
├── logs/
│   ├── observe_batch_0.log, observe_batch_1.log, ...
│   └── ... (execution logs with timings)
├── run.log (all events: stage-start, stage-subprocess-start, stage-confirmed, etc.)
└── run_summary.json (final status, stage results, finalization reason)
```

**Run log format** (line 392):
- Events: `run-start`, `stage-start`, `stage-subprocess-start`, `stage-confirmed`, `stage-failed`, `run-finished`
- JSON-compatible structured logging

**Run summary** (lines 539-565):
```json
{
  "created_at": "ISO timestamp",
  "run_stamp": "YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS",
  "runner": "codex",
  "stages_requested": ["observe", "reflect", ...],
  "stage_results": {
    "observe": {"status": "confirmed", "elapsed_seconds": 45},
    ...
  },
  "finalized": true|false,
  "finalization_reason": "partial_stage_run|completion_blocked|..."
}
```

---

### 9. **Exit Conditions & Auto-Completion**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline.py` (lines 480-525)

Auto-completion requires:
1. **Full stage run**: All 5 stages in `stages_to_run`
2. **All stages successful**: Each stage status is `confirmed` or `skipped`
3. **Completion command succeeds**: `_complete_pipeline()` succeeds

If conditions not met, pipeline exits with message:
```
Stages complete, triage not finalized ({reason}).
Finalize manually: desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "..." --attestation "..."
```

**Finalization reasons:**
- `partial_stage_run`: Only subset of stages requested
- `completion_blocked`: Auto-completion command failed (validation issues)

---

### 10. **Error Handling & Retry**

**Codex subprocess runner** (via `runner_process.py`):

Uses `CodexBatchRunnerDeps` dataclass with:
- `timeout_seconds` (default 1800s)
- `max_retries` (default 1)
- `retry_backoff_seconds` (exponential backoff: 5s, 10s, ...)
- `live_log_interval_seconds` (heartbeat every 10s)
- `stall_after_output_seconds` (timeout if no output for 120s)
- `use_popen_runner` (use Popen for better streaming)

Retry logic in `run_codex_batch()` (lines 64-133):
- Retries on timeout/stall
- Recovers if output validates even if exit code non-zero
- Logs all attempts

---

### 12. **Key Takeaways**

1. **Two execution modes**: 
   - **Codex (automated)**: Fully automated pipeline with subprocess orchestration
   - **Claude (manual)**: Interactive instructions for human agent to orchestrate

2. **Agent runner contract**: Simple — take prompt + repo_root, run subprocess, output to file, return exit code

3. **Pluggable orchestration**: StageHandler pattern allows swapping execution logic (parallel vs. serial, Codex vs. other LLMs)

4. **Stage validation is strict**: Each stage has specific gates; validation must pass before confirmation

5. **Parallel execution is first-class**: Observe and sense-check split into multiple batches and execute concurrently with thread pool

6. **Attestation is auto-generated**: Orchestrator builds stage-appropriate attestations referencing real data (dimension names, cluster names)

7. **Run artifacts are comprehensive**: All prompts, outputs, logs, and summary saved for auditability and debugging

8. **No backward compatibility for imports**: Old import paths removed; only data migration preserved (per CLAUDE.md policy)
